I mean, I haven't made any kind of plan to actually hurt myself or anything like that, but... those thoughts are just there, and it's starting to bother me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not waking up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I'd say... probably about three months now. This whole low spell has just been hanging on since that crash I had back then. The thoughts just kind of started up then and haven't really left.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I just... I quit caring about the things that usually settle me down. I usually like going fishing, digging through vinyl records, tailgating, or checking on my home-brew. But lately, all of that just feels like a chore. I just don't have the energy or interest for any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say so, no. I mean, I sit around the house a lot more these days, but I'm not pacing the floors or feeling jittery. And I don't feel like I'm moving through mud or moving in slow motion, either. Physically, I guess I feel pretty regular... I just don't have the drive to actually get up and do much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I have. My jeans are definitely fitting a lot looser these days. Most days lately, I actually have to remind myself to eat. I'll make supper, but then it just sits there on the plate... it just looks like too much trouble to even try to eat it. So, yeah, I've definitely lost some weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of two years or more where you felt depressed or low most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. I mean, I've had my rough patches here and there over the years, sure, but never anything stretching on for two whole years. It's really just been lately that I've been feeling this low.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced an episode like this before, where you felt depressed or had thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, like I said, I've had times where I felt down for a few days, but nothing like this. I've never felt this... empty before, or had those kinds of thoughts about just wanting it all to end. This is the first time it's ever gotten this bad.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It's really just been lately that I've been feeling this low.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced an episode like this before, where you felt depressed or had thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... I came in because I've been having these thoughts lately. I just keep catching myself thinking that it might be easier if I just didn't wake up in the morning. I mean, I haven't made any kind of plan to actually hurt myself or anything like that, but... those thoughts are just there, and it's starting to bother me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not waking up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I'd say... probably about three months now. This whole low spell has just been hanging on since that crash I had back then. I just don't have the energy or interest for any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say so, no. I mean, I sit around the house a lot more these days, but I'm not pacing the floors or feeling jittery. And I don't feel like I'm moving through mud or moving in slow motion, either. Physically, I guess I feel pretty regular... I just don't have the drive to actually get up and do much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I have. My jeans are definitely fitting a lot looser these days. Most days lately, I actually have to remind myself to eat. I'll make supper, but then it just sits there on the plate... it just looks like too much trouble to even try to eat it. So, yeah, I've definitely lost some weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of two years or more where you felt depressed or low most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. I mean, I've had my rough patches here and there over the years, sure, but never anything stretching on for two whole years. It's really just been lately that I've been feeling this low.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced an episode like this before, where you felt depressed or had thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, like I said, I've had times where I felt down for a few days, but nothing like this. I've never felt this... empty before, or had those kinds of thoughts about just wanting it all to end. This is the first time it's ever gotten this bad.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I've just quit caring about the things that usually settle me down. Like, I used to love going fishing, or spending a Saturday digging through old vinyl records at the shop. I even used to enjoy tailgating before the games, or just checking on my home-brew in the garage. But lately... I don't know, none of it sounds good. It all just feels like a chore now, so I don't bother.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still give you some pleasure, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was just saying, the stuff that used to give me a little bit of peace—you know, heading out to the lake to fish, flipping through records, the tailgating, the home-brew... it just doesn't do anything for me right now. Even for a minute. It all just feels like work, so I just... don't do it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low, exactly?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's been about three months now. Ever since that crash, this low spell has just been hanging on.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you no longer take pleasure in, and if so, when did you first notice this change?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned before, it's the fishing, the record shopping, tailgating, and my home-brew. I just quit caring about all of it. They used to settle me down, but now they just feel like chores. And as for when I noticed it... it was right around the same time this whole low spell started, about three months ago.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode of feeling this low in your lifetime?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I've had bad days before, sure, like anyone else. But nothing like this... this heavy feeling that just won't lift. This is the first time it's been this bad and lasted this long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of significant depression before this one that lasted for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, I've never had anything last for two weeks before this. I mean, maybe a day or two of feeling down, but never a whole two weeks straight. This stretch right now is definitely the first time it's ever been like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other episodes of depression in your life that were similar to what you're experiencing now?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I really haven't. Like I just said, this is the first time I've ever felt this way. I've never had a low spell like this before in my life.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other episodes of feeling depressed or low that were similar to what you're experiencing now, before this one started three months ago?\n\nPatient: I feel like I'm repeating myself here, doc, but no. Before this started three months ago, I was fine. I've never gone through anything like this before in my life.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any episodes of depression that were similar to this one before this one started three months ago?\n\nPatient: Doc, I... I really don't know how else to answer that. Like I've been saying, no. I haven't. Before three months ago, I never had a low spell like this. This is the very first time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
